# Full plan — MSIX package + Partner Center publishing (RunCat Lumina)

**Context:** You're registered in Partner Center already. Builds happen on Windows (local script) + GitHub Actions. App: Python tray app → PyInstaller onefile exe → MSIX payload (full-trust desktop bridge).

## Key decisions (made)

- **Package identity:** `Name=AlAsarJadeed.RunCatLumina`, `Version=1.0.0.0` (from `VERSION`, 4th part appended), `Publisher` passed as a parameter — local builds use `CN=AL ASAR JADEED`, final Store build uses your exact Partner Center Publisher string (mismatch = upload rejected; #1 failure mode).
- **Architecture:** x64 only for v1.0.0. Store build uses a **separate spec**: `console=False` (no console window), `upx=False` (UPX packing triggers AV false positives in certification).
- **No app code changes required:** config lives in `~/.config` (writable under MSIX), lock files in `%TEMP%` (fine), tray icon works in full-trust packages.

## Phase 1 — Packaging files I create (on Linux, now)

1. **`packaging/windows/msix/AppxManifest.xml`** — templated manifest:
   - `EntryPoint="Windows.fullTrustApplication"`, `Executable="RunCat-Lumina.exe"`
   - `uap:VisualElements` + assets: Square44x44, Square150x150, Wide310x150, StoreLogo
   - `rescap:runFullTrust` capability; `TargetDeviceFamily Windows.Desktop MinVersion=10.0.17763.0`
   - Publisher/Version substituted at build time
2. **`packaging/windows/msix/Assets/`** — Store logo PNGs generated from `resources/icon.png` (Pillow): 50×50, 44×44, 150×150, 310×150
3. **`packaging/windows/build-msix.ps1`** — one-command pipeline: PyInstaller (store spec) → stage dir (exe + manifest + assets) → `makeappx pack` → `signtool sign` with a generated self-signed cert → `dist\msix\RunCatLumina-1.0.0.0-x64.msix`. Params: `-Publisher`, `-Version`, `-SdkDir` (auto-detects Windows SDK).
4. **`scripts/windows/build-msix.bat`** — wrapper (matches existing script conventions)
5. **`.github/workflows/msix.yml`** — `windows-latest`: run tests, install deps, build `.msix`, upload artifact (rebuild with Partner Center publisher via workflow input)
6. **`RunCat-Lumina.store.spec`** — windowed, UPX off; existing spec untouched

## Phase 2 — Local build & validation (you, on Windows)

- Prereqs: Python 3.9+ x64, `pip install pyinstaller`, Windows SDK (VS 2022 Build Tools → Windows SDK / MSIX Packaging Tools — provides `makeappx.exe`, `signtool.exe`)
- Run `scripts\windows\build-msix.bat`
- Trust the test cert (`Import-Certificate` → `LocalMachine\TrustedPeople`), enable **Developer Mode**, `Add-AppxPackage` the `.msix`
- Verify: Start Menu entry, animated tray cat, tray-click Settings, relaunch opens Settings, uninstall removes cleanly

## Phase 3 — Partner Center prep (you)

- Copy your exact **Publisher string** (Partner Center → Account settings → publisher/identity details)
- Reserve product name **"RunCat Lumina"**
- New product → **App** → **MSIX or PWA app** (the choice you asked about)

## Phase 4 — Listing content & assets (I draft, you upload)

- **I generate:** 512×512 product logo from `resources/icon.png`, `docs/STORE-LISTING.md` with paste-ready short/long description, What's new, keywords, support URL, copyright, and a minimal privacy-policy text (app collects no data — still include a policy URL)
- **You capture:** 4–6 screenshots ≥1280×720 (settings window with color presets + live preview, dark/light tray icons, CPU tooltip)
- Form answers: category *Utilities & tools* / *Personalization*, price **Free**, age-ratings questionnaire (suitable for all ages)

## Phase 5 — Submission (you, step-by-step in `docs/STORE-SUBMISSION.md`)

1. Rebuild final package: `build-msix.ps1 -Publisher "CN=<your Partner Center publisher>"` (self-signed signature is fine — Partner Center replaces it with Microsoft's Store signature)
2. Partner Center: upload `.msix` → product properties → store listing → pricing (free, all markets)
3. **Certification notes (critical for a tray app):** *"App runs in the notification area — after launch, look for the animated cat in the system tray; double-clicking it (or launching the app again) opens the Settings window."* Otherwise the reviewer may report "app doesn't launch."
4. Submit → certification **1–7 days** (typically 1–3) → resolve any issues in the *Resolved issues* tab

## Phase 6 — Post-publish

- Updates: bump `VERSION`, CI/local build, new submission with same flow (Partner Center requires a higher package version each time)
- Optional later: ARM64 build, `StartupTask` declaration for login autostart via the package

## Risks handled

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| Publisher string mismatch | `-Publisher` parameter; final build uses Partner Center value |
| AV false positives in review | UPX disabled in store spec |
| Reviewer sees "nothing happens" (tray-only app) | Explicit certification notes; optional `--settings` flag to auto-open the window |
| Console window flashing | `console=False` store spec |
| Policy compliance | No admin/elevation, clean MSIX uninstall, Apache-2.0 sprite attribution in listing |

**Deliverables:** manifest, assets, PS1 + BAT build scripts, CI workflow, store spec, `docs/PARTNER-CENTER.md`, `docs/STORE-LISTING.md`, `docs/STORE-SUBMISSION.md`, DEPLOY.md update. **Verified by:** tests pass, manifest validates, Windows build produces an installable `.msix`, Partner Center upload accepts the package.

Switch to an agent and I'll implement Phase 1 (all repo files) — Phases 2–5 are guided steps you run with the docs I generate.

```text
"""
Test runner for RunCat Lumina

import subprocess
import sys
import os
from pathlib import Path


def run_test_file(test_file):
    """Run a single test file and return success status"""
    print(f"\n{'='*50}")
    print(f"Running {test_file.name}")
    print('='*50)

    try:
        result = subprocess.run(
            [sys.executable, str(test_file)],
            cwd=test_file.parent.parent,
            capture_output=True,
            text=True,
            timeout=30
        )

        print(result.stdout)
        if result.stderr:
            print("STDERR:", result.stderr)

        return result.returncode == 0
```

`DEPLOY.md` and `README.md` updated with the Store section.

**Publishing — 5 steps**

1. **Partner Center → Account settings** → copy your exact `CN=...` Publisher ID
2. **Build:** `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File packaging\windows\build-msix.ps1 -Publisher "CN=<that string>"` (needs Windows SDK; or run the `msix` GitHub workflow and download the artifact)
3. **Sanity test:** import the `.cer` → enable Developer Mode → `Add-AppxPackage` → tray cat + Settings work
4. **Submit:** Apps & games → + New app → App → **MSIX or PWA app** → upload `dist\msix\RunCatLumina-1.0.0.0-x64.msix` → paste listing from `docs/STORE-LISTING.md` → free/all markets → paste the tray-app certification note (in `STORE-LISTING.md` — it prevents the "nothing launches" rejection) → Submit
5. **Certification:** 1–3 days typical; check **Resolved issues** if flagged, then find *RunCat Lumina* in the Store

Full walkthrough with troubleshooting table: `docs/STORE-SUBMISSION.md`. Also publish `docs/STORE-PRIVACY-POLICY.md` at `alasarjadeed.com/privacy` before submitting — the listing needs that URL.

Changes are uncommitted; say the word if you want me to commit them.

```bash
import json, time, urllib.request

API = "https://api.github.com/repos/alasrjadeed/RunCat-Lumina"

def get(path):
    req = urllib.request.Request(API + path, headers={"User-Agent": "verify", "Accept": "application/vnd.github+json"})
    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30) as r:
        return json.load(r)

def runs():
    return get("/actions/runs?per_page=20")["workflow_runs"]

def report(rs):
    lines = []
    for r in rs:
        if r.get("head_branch") == "v1.0.1" or (r.get("head_sha", "").startswith("5aaa34a")):
            lines.append(f"  {r['name']:<18} {r['event']:<8} {r['status']:<12} {r.get('conclusion')}")
    return lines

for i in range(16):
    rs = [r for r in runs() if r.get("head_sha", "").startswith("5aaa34a")]
    lines = [f"  {r['name']:<18} {r['event']:<8} {r['status']:<12} {r.get('conclusion')}" for r in rs]
    done = rs and all(r["status"] == "completed" for r in rs)
    print(f"[{i}] " + ("\n    ".join(lines) if lines else "waiting for runs to appear..."), flush=True)
    if done:
        break
    time.sleep(30)

print("\nRelease:")
try:
    rel = get("/releases/tags/v1.0.1")
    print("  tag:", rel["tag_name"], "| name:", rel
… [215 more chars]
```

Fix properly in the test runner (repo code, not just CI env):


2. Also avoid non-ASCII markers by default? Keeping ✓ but with UTF-8 reconfigure works; however the *subprocess* test output could also contain non-ASCII (test files may print unicode). The subprocess inherits env; on Windows the child's stdout is a pipe (captured by `capture_output=True`) → text=True decodes with locale encoding (cp1252) → if a child prints UTF-8 unicode, decoding could fail or mangle. Passing `encoding="utf-8"` to subprocess.run would be more robust... but the child writes with its own encoding (cp1252 pipes). Apparently not: the array elements are bound positionally in order, and strings starting with `-` are NOT interpreted as parameter names in this context (they're treated as values). That's the gotcha: splatting an array does NOT re-parse `-Name` tokens as parameter names. Known PowerShell behavior: array splatting passes values positionally; to pass named parameters you must use a **hashtable** splat.

So fix: use a hashtable splat:
```powershell
$scriptParams = @{ Publisher = $pub }
if ($ver) { $scriptParams['Version'] = $ver }
./packaging/windows/build-msix.ps1 @scriptParams
```
Hashtable splatting binds by name ✓. That's the documented approach.

So my build-msix.ps1 version-normalization bug: 

```powershell
if (-not $Version) {
    $raw = (Get-Content (Join-Path $Root 'VERSION') -Raw).Trim()
    $Version = if ($raw -match '^\d+\.\d+\.\d+$') { "$raw.0" } else { $raw }
}
```

When Version is passed explicitly (from the workflow), the normalization block is skipped — so a 3-part "1.0.1" passed in fails. Fix: normalize whenever Version has 3 parts, i.e. restructure:

```powershell
if (-not $Version) {
    $Version = (Get-Content (Join-Path $Root 'VERSION') -Raw).Trim()
}
if ($Version -match '^\d+\.\d+\.\d+$') { $Version = "$Version.0" }
if ($Version -notmatch '^\d+\.\d+\.\d+\.\d+$') { throw ... }
```

1. Install Python 3 and venv, for example:

```bash
bash scripts/linux/install.sh
```

```bash
bash scripts/linux/start.sh
```

Uninstall:

bash scripts/linux/uninstall.sh

## 🤖 Assistant — 2026-10-16 13:50

Linux and macOS packaging is done and live on the GitHub release.

**Release v1.0.1** (https://github.com/alasrjadeed/RunCat-Lumina/releases/tag/v1.0.1) now carries:
| Asset | Size | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `RunCatLumina-1.0.1-Setup.exe` | 20.5 MB | Windows installer (unchanged) |
| `RunCatLumina-1.0.1-linux-x86_64.tar.gz` | 33.3 MB | standalone binary, no Python needed |
| `RunCatLumina-1.0.1-macos-arm64.dmg` | 17.6 MB | real `.app` bundle, drag-to-Applications |

All three verified anonymously downloadable (HTTP 206).

**What changed**
- `packaging/linux/` — PyInstaller spec + `build-linux.sh`; CI smoke-tests the binary under Xvfb (kills it after 10s, fails unless it stayed up).
- `packaging/macos/` — menu-bar-only `.app` spec (`LSUIElement`), `make_icon.py` (iconset → `.icns` via `iconutil`), `build-macos.sh` producing a UDZO DMG.
- `scripts/linux/install.sh` — detects a bundled `RunCat-Lumina` binary and installs it without a venv, falling back to the source/venv path otherwise.
- `.github/workflows/release-unix.yml` — `ubuntu-22.04` (glibc 2.35 for older distros) + `macos-latest` builds, tests on both, then a publish job that uploads to the tag's release and force-un-drafts it.
- README/DEPLOY document both new download paths; `.gitignore` covers the new build dirs.

**Three CI bugs found and fixed from job logs:** `iconutil` requires the iconset dir to end in `.iconset`; `--target-arch` is a makespec-only flag so it moved into the spec via `PYI_TARGET_ARCH`; universal2 is impossible since MRI wheels per-arch (Pillow `_avif.so` isn't fat), so the DMG is native arm64.

**Caveat:** the DMG is Apple-Silicon only (the runner has no x86_64 Python), and the app is ad-hoc signed, not notarized — first launch needs right-click → Open. Intel Macs use the source install; both are spelled out in `DEPLOY.md`.
